In medium-chain ADHs, the catalytic zinc is buried in the active site, while the structural zinc is at the interface between domains. The structural zinc is coordinated by 4 Cys residues that are close in the tertiary structure but spread out in sequence.

Here&#x27;s my reasoning: In this particular enzyme, C112 may serve as the third ligand for the catalytic zinc (replacing the canonical Ser). This would make C101, C104, and C154 the structural zinc ligands (with possibly a water molecule as the fourth). The &quot;enzyme-specific&quot; aspect the question mentions might refer to this non-canonical arrangement where C112 is catalytic rather than structural.

Alternatively, if the structural zinc is C101, C104, C112, C154, then C112 can&#x27;t be catalytic. In that case, B would be the answer with C154 as the third catalytic ligand (which seems less likely given the spacing).

I&#x27;ll go with D as my final answer, as C112 being adjacent to C104 in the CxxC motif could potentially serve either role depending on the enzyme, and in some ADHs, the catalytic and structural zinc sites can have different assignments than the canonical horse liver ADH.
